                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Markdown Content:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'A tutorial on how to use the useSyncedState hook for realtime shared state.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'RedwoodSDK provides built-in support for real-time applications through shared state synchronization. The primary way to implement this is via the `useSyncedState` hook.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"`useSyncedState` looks exactly like React's native `useState`, except it has bidirectional syncing with the server and all other connected clients.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"*   It's a hook that synchronizes state across multiple clients (tabs, devices, users) in real-time.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'*   The server is the source of truth.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'*   It allows you to build collaborative features without needing an external realtime service.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'*   **Realtime**: Updates are instant for all users on the page.\n'[39m +
                      [32m"*   **Native**: It's built into RedwoodSDK.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'*   **Cloudflare**: It leverages Cloudflare Durable Objects for coordination.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'*   Any component where you want data to update instantly for everyone.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'*   Examples: Chat apps, collaborative forms, live dashboards, presence indicators.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'This is a low-level primitive. Currently, all values are stored in **memory** within the Durable Object. If the Durable Object is evicted or the worker restarts, the state is wiped. However, you can add callbacks to persist data to a database if needed.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Here is the easiest way to get started.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### [1. Setup the Worker](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#1-setup-the-worker)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'In your `src/worker.tsx`, you need to export the `SyncedStateServer` (the Durable Object) and register its routes.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { env } from "cloudflare:workers";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  SyncedStateServer,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  syncedStateRoutes,\n'[39m +
                      [32m'} from "rwsdk/use-synced-state/worker";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { defineApp } from "rwsdk/worker";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'// 1. Export the Durable Object so Cloudflare can find it\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export { SyncedStateServer };\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export default defineApp([\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ... your other middleware\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // 2. Register the synced state routes\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ...syncedStateRoutes(() => env.SYNCED_STATE_SERVER),\n'[39m +
                      [32m']);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### [2. Update Wrangler Config](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#2-update-wrangler-config)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You need to tell Cloudflare about the Durable Object. Add the following to your `wrangler.jsonc`:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"durable_objects": {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  "bindings": [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      "name": "SYNCED_STATE_SERVER",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      "class_name": "SyncedStateServer"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  ]\n'[39m +
                      [32m'},\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"migrations": [\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    "tag": "v1",\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    "new_sqlite_classes": ["SyncedStateServer"]\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m']\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'> **Note**: After changing `wrangler.jsonc`, run `pnpm generate` to update your types.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### [3. Use the Hook](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#3-use-the-hook)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Now you can use `useSyncedState` in your components. It works just like `useState`, but takes a second argument: a unique key, and an optional third argument: a room ID.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"use client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useSyncedState } from "rwsdk/use-synced-state/client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const SharedCounter = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // "counter" is the unique key for this piece of state\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Without a room ID, this state is global across all clients\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const [count, setCount] = useSyncedState(0, "counter");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    <div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <p>Count: {count}</p>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <button onClick={() => setCount((c) => c + 1)}>Increment</button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    </div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  );\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Open this component in two different browser windows. When you click increment in one, it updates in the other instantly!\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### [Rooms: Scoping State to Different Groups](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#rooms-scoping-state-to-different-groups)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'By default, state is global. But you can scope state to different "rooms" by passing a room ID as the third argument. This is useful for features like chat rooms, game sessions, or collaborative documents.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"use client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useSyncedState } from "rwsdk/use-synced-state/client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const RoomCounter = ({ roomId }: { roomId: string }) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // State is scoped to this specific room\n'[39m +
                      [32m"  // Users in different rooms won't see each other's updates\n"[39m +
                      [32m'  const [count, setCount] = useSyncedState(0, "counter", roomId);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return (\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    <div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <p>Room: {roomId}</p>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <p>Count: {count}</p>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      <button onClick={() => setCount((c) => c + 1)}>Increment</button>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    </div>\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  );\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'When you use a room ID, state is isolated to that room. Users in `"room-1"` won\'t see updates from users in `"room-2"`.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'* * *\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### [Scoping State with Room IDs vs Key Handlers](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#scoping-state-with-room-ids-vs-key-handlers)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'There are two ways to scope state:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'1.   **Room IDs** (client-side): Pass a room ID as the third argument to `useSyncedState`. This is the simplest way to isolate state between different groups.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'2.   **Key Handlers** (server-side): Transform keys on the server to add prefixes or scoping logic. This is useful when you need server-enforced scoping based on authentication or other server-side data.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### [Using Room IDs (Client-Side)](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#using-room-ids-client-side)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"use client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useSyncedState } from "rwsdk/use-synced-state/client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const ChatRoom = ({ roomId }: { roomId: string }) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Each room has its own isolated state\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const [messages, setMessages] = useSyncedState<string[]>([], "messages", roomId);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  \n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ... chat UI\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### [Using Key Handlers (Server-Side)](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#using-key-handlers-server-side)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Key handlers allow you to transform keys on the server, which is useful for server-enforced scoping:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { requestInfo } from "rwsdk/worker";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'SyncedStateServer.registerKeyHandler(async (key, stub) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Access user ID from request context\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const userId = requestInfo.ctx.userId;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  \n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Scope keys that start with "user:" to the current user\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  if (key.startsWith("user:")) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    return `${key}:${userId}`;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  \n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return key;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Then in your component:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"use client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useSyncedState } from "rwsdk/use-synced-state/client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const UserSettings = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // The key handler will transform this to "user:settings:123" (where 123 is the userId)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Each user gets their own isolated settings\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const [settings, setSettings] = useSyncedState({}, "user:settings");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  \n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ... settings UI\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'#### [Server-Side Room Transformation](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#server-side-room-transformation)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'You can also transform room IDs on the server using a room handler. This is useful for features like "private" rooms that should be scoped per user:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { requestInfo } from "rwsdk/worker";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'SyncedStateServer.registerRoomHandler(async (roomId, reqInfo) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const userId = reqInfo?.ctx?.userId;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  \n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Transform "private" room requests to user-specific rooms\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  if (roomId === "private" && userId) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    return `user:${userId}`;\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  \n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Pass through other room IDs as-is\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  return roomId ?? "syncedState";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Then clients can request a "private" room, and the server will automatically scope it to the current user:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"use client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useSyncedState } from "rwsdk/use-synced-state/client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const PrivateNotes = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // Server transforms "private" to "user:${userId}" automatically\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const [notes, setNotes] = useSyncedState("", "notes", "private");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  \n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ... notes UI\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### [Persisting State](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#persisting-state)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Since the state is in-memory, you might want to save it to a database. You can register handlers for when state is set or retrieved.\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'SyncedStateServer.registerSetStateHandler((key, value) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  console.log("State updated:", key, value);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // db.save(key, value);\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'SyncedStateServer.registerGetStateHandler((key, value) => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'   // potentially load from DB if value is undefined\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'`useSyncedState` automatically handles dropped WebSocket connections. If the connection dies (bad wifi, server restart, etc.), it will:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'1.   Detect the broken connection\n'[39m +
                      [32m'2.   Reconnect with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s... up to 30s)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'3.   Re-subscribe all active state keys\n'[39m +
                      [32m'4.   Fetch the latest state so your UI catches up on missed updates\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m"This happens transparently — your components don't need any changes.\n"[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'### [Connection Status Callback](https://docs.rwsdk.com/experimental/realtime/#connection-status-callback)\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'If you want to show a banner or toast when the connection drops, use the `onStatusChange` callback:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { createSyncedStateHook } from "rwsdk/use-synced-state/client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const useSyncedState = createSyncedStateHook({\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  onStatusChange(status) {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    // status: "connected" | "disconnected" | "reconnecting"\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    if (status === "disconnected") {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      console.warn("Connection lost, reconnecting...");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    if (status === "connected") {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'      console.log("Connection restored");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'    }\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  },\n'[39m +
                      [32m'});\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'Then use your custom hook instead of the default:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'"use client";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'import { useSyncedState } from "@/app/hooks";\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'export const SharedCounter = () => {\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  const [count, setCount] = useSyncedState(0, "counter");\n'[39m +
                      [32m'  // ...\n'[39m +
                      [32m'};\n'[39m +
                      [32m'```\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'We are working on making this even more powerful out of the box:\n'[39m +
                      [32m'\n'[39m +
                      [32m'*   **Offline Support**: Local persistence (e.g., via IndexedDB) so your app works offline and syncs changes when the connection is restored.\n'[39m +
                      [32m"*   **Durable Storage**: Built-in persistence to the Durable Object's SQLite storage, ensuring state survives worker restarts without custom handlers.\n"[39m +
                      [32m''[39m

            [32m'# Committing changes with git\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'Only create commits when requested by the user. If unclear, ask first. When the user asks you to create a new git commit, follow these steps carefully:\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'Git Safety Protocol:\n'[39m +
            [32m'- NEVER update the git config\n'[39m +
            [32m"- NEVER run destructive git commands (push --force, reset --hard, checkout ., restore ., clean -f, branch -D) unless the user explicitly requests these actions. Taking unauthorized destructive actions is unhelpful and can result in lost work, so it's best to ONLY run these commands when given direct instructions \n"[39m +
            [32m'- NEVER skip hooks (--no-verify, --no-gpg-sign, etc) unless the user explicitly requests it\n'[39m +
            [32m'- NEVER run force push to main/master, warn the user if they request it\n'[39m +
            [32m'- CRITICAL: Always create NEW commits rather than amending, unless the user explicitly requests a git amend. When a pre-commit hook fails, the commit did NOT happen  — so --amend would modify the PREVIOUS commit, which may result in destroying work or losing previous changes. Instead, after hook failure, fix the issue, re-stage, and create a NEW commit\n'[39m +
            [32m'- When staging files, prefer adding specific files by name rather than using "git add -A" or "git add .", which can accidentally include sensitive files (.env, credentials) or large binaries\n'[39m +

            [32m'\n'[39m +

            [32m'  - Run a git status command to see all untracked files. IMPORTANT: Never use the -uall flag as it can cause memory issues on large repos.\n'[39m +
            [32m'  - Run a git diff command to see both staged and unstaged changes that will be committed.\n'[39m +
            [32m"  - Run a git log command to see recent commit messages, so that you can follow this repository's commit message style.\n"[39m +
            [32m'2. Analyze all staged changes (both previously staged and newly added) and draft a commit message:\n'[39m +

            [32m'  - Do not commit files that likely contain secrets (.env, credentials.json, etc). Warn the user if they specifically request to commit those files\n'[39m +
